10. Which is correct: "a lot of homework" or "alot of homework"? Answer: "a lot of homework." Hint: Find the phrase that means a large amount, and check whether it should be written as one word or two.
11. Fix the spelling: "It rained alot last week." Answer: "It rained a lot last week." Hint: Find the phrase that means a large amount, and check whether it should be written as one word or two.
12. Fix the spelling: "There were alot of birds at the lake." Answer: "There were a lot of birds at the lake." Hint: Find the phrase that means a large amount, and check whether it should be written as one word or two.

### 13.6 "Their" vs. "There" vs. "They're"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Their shows that something belongs to them. There points to a place, or starts a sentence about something existing. They're is short for they are.
- Example: "Their dog is friendly." Their shows the dog belongs to them. "The dog is over there." There points to a place. "They're going to the park." They're means they are.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. Belonging means their. A place or an existing thing means there. They are means they're.
- Tip box: "Their house is big." Belonging, so their. "Put the book there." A place, so there. "They're happy today." They are, so they're.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "___ dog is friendly." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: Their. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
2. Fill in the blank: "The dog is over ___." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: there. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ going to the park." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: They're. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
4. Fill in the blank: "___ house is big." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: Their. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
5. Fill in the blank: "Put the book ___." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: there. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
6. Fill in the blank: "___ happy today." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: They're. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ team won the game." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: Their. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
8. Fill in the blank: "Please wait for me right ___." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: there. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
9. Fill in the blank: "___ the best players in the league." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: They're. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
10. Fill in the blank: "___ teacher gave them extra time." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: Their. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
11. Fill in the blank: "The keys are ___ on the table." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: there. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.
12. Fill in the blank: "___ going to win the race." Choose their, there, or they're. Answer: They're. Hint: Ask what the word does here: show belonging, point to a place, or stand for they are.

## Pathway 14: Commonly Confused Words II (8 topics)

### 14.1 "Knot" vs. "Not"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Knot is a fastening made by tying rope, string, or thread. Not makes a sentence negative.
- Example: "Tie a knot in the rope." Knot is the fastening. "I am not tired." Not makes the sentence negative.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. A tied fastening means knot. Making something negative means not.
- Tip box: "The sailor tied a tight knot." Knot is the fastening. "She did not forget." Not makes the sentence negative.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "Tie a ___ in the rope." Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
2. Fill in the blank: "I am ___ tired." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
3. Fill in the blank: "The sailor tied a tight ___." Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
4. Fill in the blank: "She did ___ forget her lunch." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
5. Fill in the blank: "Can you untie this ___?" Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
6. Fill in the blank: "It is ___ raining today." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
7. Fill in the blank: "The scout learned to tie a square ___." Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
8. Fill in the blank: "We are ___ going to the fair." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
9. Fill in the blank: "Pull the ___ tight so it holds." Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
10. Fill in the blank: "Do ___ leave your bag here." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
11. Fill in the blank: "The ribbon had a pretty ___." Choose knot or not. Answer: knot. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.
12. Fill in the blank: "He could ___ find his keys." Choose knot or not. Answer: not. Hint: Ask whether the word names a tied fastening or makes the sentence negative.

### 14.2 "Who's" vs. "Whose"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Who's is short for who is or who has. Whose shows that something belongs to someone.
- Example: "Who's coming to the party?" Who's means who is. "Whose jacket is this?" Whose asks who the jacket belongs to.
- How it works: Try replacing the word with who is. If the sentence still makes sense, use who's with an apostrophe. If not, use whose with no apostrophe.
- Tip box: "Who's at the door?" Who is at the door, so use who's. "Whose turn is it?" The turn belongs to someone, so use whose.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "___ coming to the party?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
2. Fill in the blank: "___ jacket is this?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ at the door?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
4. Fill in the blank: "___ turn is it?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
5. Fill in the blank: "___ been to the new library?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
6. Fill in the blank: "___ book is on the desk?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ going to win the race?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
8. Fill in the blank: "___ bike is parked outside?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
9. Fill in the blank: "___ the fastest runner?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
10. Fill in the blank: "___ idea was it to build the fort?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
11. Fill in the blank: "___ finished the puzzle already?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Who's. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.
12. Fill in the blank: "___ lunchbox is this?" Choose who's or whose. Answer: Whose. Hint: Try reading the sentence with who is or who has in the blank. If that makes sense, you need the apostrophe. If the word asks who something belongs to, you do not.

### 14.3 "Right" vs. "Write"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Right can mean correct, or the side opposite left. Write means to form letters and words on paper or a screen.
- Example: "That is the right answer." Right means correct. "Please write your name." Write means to form letters.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. Correct or a side means right. Forming letters means write.
- Tip box: "Turn right at the corner." Right is a direction. "Write a story about a dragon." Write means to form words.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "That is the ___ answer." Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
2. Fill in the blank: "Please ___ your name on the paper." Choose right or write. Answer: write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
3. Fill in the blank: "Turn ___ at the corner." Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
4. Fill in the blank: "___ a story about a dragon." Choose Right or Write. Answer: Write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
5. Fill in the blank: "Raise your ___ hand." Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
6. Fill in the blank: "Did you ___ the letter yet?" Choose right or write. Answer: write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
7. Fill in the blank: "You made the ___ choice." Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
8. Fill in the blank: "She likes to ___ poems." Choose right or write. Answer: write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
9. Fill in the blank: "The ___ side of the road is closed." Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
10. Fill in the blank: "___ your answers in pen." Choose Right or Write. Answer: Write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
11. Fill in the blank: "Is this the ___ way to the station?" Choose right or write. Answer: right. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.
12. Fill in the blank: "He will ___ a book one day." Choose right or write. Answer: write. Hint: Ask whether the word means correct or a side, or means forming letters and words.

### 14.4 "Passed" vs. "Past"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Passed is the past tense of pass. It means to go by, to hand over, or to succeed. Past means time that has gone by, or a position beyond something.
- Example: "We passed the library on our walk." Passed is the verb. "In the past, the town had one school." Past is a noun about time gone by.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. A verb about going by or succeeding means passed. Time gone by or a position beyond means past.
- Tip box: "She passed the test." Passed is the verb. "Walk past the gate." Past tells the position beyond the gate.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "We ___ the library on our walk." Choose passed or past. Answer: passed. Hint: Ask whether the word is a verb about going by, handing over, or succeeding, or whether it names time gone by or a position beyond something.

6. Fill in the blank: "The ___ week was very rainy." Choose passed or past. Answer: past. Hint: Ask whether the word is a verb about going by, handing over, or succeeding, or whether it names time gone by or a position beyond something.
7. Fill in the blank: "He ___ the ball to his teammate." Choose passed or past. Answer: passed. Hint: Ask whether the word is a verb about going by, handing over, or succeeding, or whether it names time gone by or a position beyond something.

12. Fill in the blank: "Drive ___ the bridge to reach the farm." Choose passed or past. Answer: past. Hint: Ask whether the word is a verb about going by, handing over, or succeeding, or whether it names time gone by or a position beyond something.

### 14.5 "Into" vs. "In to"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Into shows movement from outside to inside. In to is two words: in is part of the verb or place, and to starts a new phrase, often showing purpose.
- Example: "The cat jumped into the box." Into shows movement inside. "Ana came in to eat lunch." In belongs with came, and to shows purpose.
- How it works: Ask if the word shows movement inside. If yes, use into as one word. If in belongs with the verb and to starts a purpose phrase, write two words.
- Tip box: "Pour the juice into the glass." Into shows movement inside. "He turned himself in to the teacher." In belongs with turned, and to starts a new phrase.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "The cat jumped ___ the box." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
2. Fill in the blank: "Ana came ___ eat lunch." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
3. Fill in the blank: "Pour the juice ___ the glass." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
4. Fill in the blank: "He turned himself ___ the teacher." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
5. Fill in the blank: "The frog hopped ___ the pond." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
6. Fill in the blank: "She logged ___ check her homework." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
7. Fill in the blank: "The dog ran ___ the yard." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
8. Fill in the blank: "Leo went ___ buy a ticket." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
9. Fill in the blank: "Put the books ___ the bag." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
10. Fill in the blank: "Maya stopped ___ say hello." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
11. Fill in the blank: "The bird flew ___ the nest." Choose into or in to. Answer: into. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.
12. Fill in the blank: "We dropped ___ visit our cousins." Choose into or in to. Answer: in to. Hint: Look at the verb. Ask whether something moves from outside to inside, or whether in goes with the verb and to starts a new phrase.

### 14.6 "Desert" vs. "Dessert"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Desert is a dry place with little rain, or the verb meaning to leave or abandon. Dessert is a sweet food served after a meal.
- Example: "The camel lives in the desert." Desert is the dry place. "We ate cake for dessert." Dessert is the sweet food.
- How it works: Ask what the word means. A dry place or leaving means desert with one s. A sweet food after a meal means dessert with two s's.
- Tip box: "Dessert has two s's because you always want seconds." A memory trick: the sweet one has double s. "The soldier deserted the camp." Deserted means he left it.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "The camel lives in the ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
2. Fill in the blank: "We ate cake for ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
3. Fill in the blank: "The ___ is hot and dry." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
4. Fill in the blank: "Ice cream is my favorite ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
5. Fill in the blank: "Cactus plants grow in the ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
6. Fill in the blank: "What is for ___ tonight?" Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
7. Fill in the blank: "The soldier decided to ___ his post." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
8. Fill in the blank: "We had fruit salad for ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
9. Fill in the blank: "Sand dunes stretch across the ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
10. Fill in the blank: "The chef made a chocolate ___." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
11. Fill in the blank: "Do not ___ your friends when they need you." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: desert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.
12. Fill in the blank: "Pie is a popular ___ in our house." Choose desert or dessert. Answer: dessert. Hint: Ask whether the word means a dry place or leaving, or a sweet food after a meal. Remember the double s trick.

### 14.7 "Because" vs. "Cause"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Because introduces a reason. Cause is a noun meaning something that makes something else happen, or a verb meaning to make something happen.
- Example: "We stayed home because it rained." Because introduces the reason. "The rain was the cause of the flood." Cause is the noun for what made the flood happen.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. Introducing a reason means because. Naming what made something happen means cause.
- Tip box: "She was late because the bus broke down." Because gives the reason. "Careless driving can cause accidents." Cause is the verb for making something happen.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "We stayed home ___ it rained." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
2. Fill in the blank: "The rain was the ___ of the flood." Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
3. Fill in the blank: "She was late ___ the bus broke down." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
4. Fill in the blank: "Careless driving can ___ accidents." Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
5. Fill in the blank: "He smiled ___ he won the game." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
6. Fill in the blank: "What was the ___ of the fire?" Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
7. Fill in the blank: "We wore coats ___ it was cold." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
8. Fill in the blank: "Loud music can ___ headaches." Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
9. Fill in the blank: "Ana stayed up late ___ she was reading." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
10. Fill in the blank: "The ___ of the delay was a flat tire." Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
11. Fill in the blank: "They left early ___ the roads were icy." Choose because or cause. Answer: because. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.
12. Fill in the blank: "Too much sun can ___ sunburn." Choose because or cause. Answer: cause. Hint: Ask whether the word introduces a reason, or is a noun or verb about making something happen.

### 14.8 "Principal" vs. "Principle"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Principal is the head of a school, or the main or most important thing. Principle is a rule, a belief, or a basic truth.
- Example: "The principal greeted us at the door." Principal is the head of the school. "Honesty is an important principle." Principle is a rule to live by.
- How it works: Ask what the word means. A person in charge or the main thing means principal, which ends in pal. A rule or belief means principle, which ends in ple.
- Tip box: "The principal is your pal." A memory trick: principal ends in pal. "The principle of fairness guides the rules." Principle is a rule.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "The ___ greeted us at the door." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
2. Fill in the blank: "Honesty is an important ___." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
3. Fill in the blank: "The ___ reason we came is to learn." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
4. Fill in the blank: "The ___ of fairness guides the rules." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
5. Fill in the blank: "Our ___ announced the new schedule." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
6. Fill in the blank: "Kindness is a good ___ to follow." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
7. Fill in the blank: "The ___ actor in the play is Maya." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
8. Fill in the blank: "The ___ of gravity explains why things fall." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
9. Fill in the blank: "The ___'s office is near the front door." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
10. Fill in the blank: "Sharing is a ___ we practice in class." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
11. Fill in the blank: "Rice is the ___ food in many countries." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principal. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.
12. Fill in the blank: "One ___ of good writing is clear sentences." Choose principal or principle. Answer: principle. Hint: Ask whether the word means a person in charge or the main thing, or a rule, belief, or basic truth. Remember the pal trick.

## Pathway 15: Commonly Confused Words III (10 topics)

### 15.1 "Who" vs. "Whom"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Who is a subject pronoun. It does the action. Whom is an object pronoun. It receives the action.
- Example: "Who knocked on the door?" Who is the subject. "Whom did you call?" Whom is the object of call.
- How it works: Try replacing the word with he or him. If he fits, use who. If him fits, use whom.
- Tip box: "Who is coming?" He is coming, so use who. "To whom should I give this?" To him, so use whom.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "___ knocked on the door?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
2. Fill in the blank: "___ did you call?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ is coming to the party?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
4. Fill in the blank: "To ___ should I give this?" Choose who or whom. Answer: whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
5. Fill in the blank: "___ wrote this story?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
6. Fill in the blank: "___ did the teacher praise?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ will lead the team?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
8. Fill in the blank: "For ___ is this gift?" Choose who or whom. Answer: whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
9. Fill in the blank: "___ ate the last cookie?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
10. Fill in the blank: "___ did you invite?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
11. Fill in the blank: "___ knows the answer?" Choose who or whom. Answer: Who. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.
12. Fill in the blank: "With ___ did you go?" Choose who or whom. Answer: whom. Hint: Answer the question with he or him in place of the blank. If he fits, use the subject form. If him fits, use the object form.

### 15.2 "Quiet" vs. "Quite"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Quiet means silent or calm. Quite means very, or completely.
- Example: "The library is quiet." Quiet means silent. "The test was quite hard." Quite means very.
- How it works: Ask what the word means. Silent or calm means quiet. Very or completely means quite.
- Tip box: "Please be quiet." Quiet asks for silence. "I am quite tired." Quite means very tired.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "The library is ___." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
2. Fill in the blank: "The test was ___ hard." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
3. Fill in the blank: "Please be ___." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
4. Fill in the blank: "I am ___ tired." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
5. Fill in the blank: "The baby slept in a ___ room." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
6. Fill in the blank: "The movie was ___ long." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
7. Fill in the blank: "The forest was ___ at dawn." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
8. Fill in the blank: "She is ___ good at chess." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
9. Fill in the blank: "Keep your voice ___." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
10. Fill in the blank: "It is ___ cold outside." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
11. Fill in the blank: "The classroom was ___ after lunch." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quiet. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.
12. Fill in the blank: "The hike was ___ steep." Choose quiet or quite. Answer: quite. Hint: Ask whether the word means silent or calm, or means very or completely.

### 15.3 "Morning" vs. "Mourning"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Morning is the early part of the day. Mourning is sadness after someone dies.
- Example: "We eat breakfast in the morning." Morning is the early part of the day. "The town was mourning the loss of its mayor." Mourning is sadness after a death.
- How it works: Ask what the word means. The early part of the day means morning. Sadness after a death means mourning.
- Tip box: "Good morning." Morning greets the early day. "They wore black in mourning." Mourning shows grief.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "We eat breakfast in the ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
2. Fill in the blank: "The town was ___ the loss of its mayor." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
3. Fill in the blank: "Good ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
4. Fill in the blank: "They wore black in ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
5. Fill in the blank: "The sun rises in the ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
6. Fill in the blank: "The family spent a year in ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
7. Fill in the blank: "We meet at nine every ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
8. Fill in the blank: "The bells tolled in ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
9. Fill in the blank: "The ___ dew covered the grass." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
10. Fill in the blank: "The whole village was in ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
11. Fill in the blank: "She runs every ___." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: morning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.
12. Fill in the blank: "The poem speaks of ___ and loss." Choose morning or mourning. Answer: mourning. Hint: Ask whether the word names the early part of the day or sadness after someone dies.

### 15.4 "Maybe" vs. "May be"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Maybe is one word and means perhaps. May be is two words: may is a helping verb, and be is the main verb.
- Example: "Maybe we will go to the fair." Maybe means perhaps. "It may be sunny tomorrow." May be means it is possible that it will be sunny.
- How it works: Ask if the word means perhaps. If yes, use maybe as one word. If may is helping be, write two words.
- Tip box: "Maybe she will call." Maybe means perhaps. "She may be late." May is a helping verb, and be is the main verb.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "___ we will go to the fair." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
2. Fill in the blank: "It ___ sunny tomorrow." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ she will call tonight." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
4. Fill in the blank: "She ___ late to class." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
5. Fill in the blank: "___ the best player will win." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
6. Fill in the blank: "The test ___ harder than we think." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ we should bring umbrellas." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
8. Fill in the blank: "He ___ the tallest in the class." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
9. Fill in the blank: "___ the rain will stop by noon." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
10. Fill in the blank: "The answer ___ wrong." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
11. Fill in the blank: "___ we can visit the museum." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: Maybe. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.
12. Fill in the blank: "The road ___ closed after the storm." Choose maybe or may be. Answer: may be. Hint: Try putting perhaps in the blank. If it makes sense, write one word. If may is helping the verb be, write two words.

### 15.5 "Good" vs. "Well"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Good is an adjective. It describes a noun. Well is an adverb. It describes a verb. Well can also be an adjective meaning healthy.
- Example: "She is a good singer." Good describes singer, a noun. "She sings well." Well describes sings, a verb.
- How it works: Ask what the word describes. A noun takes good. A verb takes well. If someone is healthy, well is the adjective.
- Tip box: "A good book" uses good for the noun book. "He reads well" uses well for the verb reads. "I feel well today" uses well to mean healthy.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "She is a ___ singer." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
2. Fill in the blank: "She sings ___." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
3. Fill in the blank: "He is a ___ runner." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
4. Fill in the blank: "He runs ___." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
5. Fill in the blank: "It was a ___ day for a picnic." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
6. Fill in the blank: "The team played ___." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
7. Fill in the blank: "We hope Grandpa gets ___ soon after his cold." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
8. Fill in the blank: "That is a ___ idea." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
9. Fill in the blank: "Leo did ___ on the test." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
10. Fill in the blank: "Maya is a ___ friend." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
11. Fill in the blank: "The soup tastes ___." Choose good or well. Answer: good. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?
12. Fill in the blank: "She speaks French ___." Choose good or well. Answer: well. Hint: Find the word the blank describes. Is it a noun, an action verb, or a person's health?

### 15.6 "Everyday" vs. "Every day"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Everyday is one word and means ordinary or normal. Every day is two words and means each day.
- Example: "These are my everyday shoes." Everyday means ordinary. "I walk to school every day." Every day means each day.
- How it works: Ask what the words mean. Ordinary means everyday as one word. Each day means every day as two words.
- Tip box: "An everyday routine" means a normal routine. "We practice every day" means we practice each day.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "These are my ___ shoes." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
2. Fill in the blank: "I walk to school ___." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
3. Fill in the blank: "An ___ routine helps me stay on time." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
4. Fill in the blank: "We practice piano ___." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
5. Fill in the blank: "She wears an ___ dress to school." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
6. Fill in the blank: "The dog needs a walk ___." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
7. Fill in the blank: "Cooking is an ___ skill." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
8. Fill in the blank: "We water the plants ___." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
9. Fill in the blank: "He uses his ___ backpack." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
10. Fill in the blank: "She reads ___ before bed." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
11. Fill in the blank: "An ___ event is not special." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: everyday. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.
12. Fill in the blank: "The mail comes ___ except Sunday." Choose everyday or every day. Answer: every day. Hint: Ask whether the word means ordinary, or means each day.

### 15.7 "Everyone" vs. "Every one"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Everyone is one word and means all the people. Every one is two words and means each single thing or person in a group.
- Example: "Everyone in class passed the test." Everyone means all the people. "Every one of the books was checked." Every one means each single book.
- How it works: Ask what the words mean. All the people means everyone as one word. Each single item in a group means every one as two words.
- Tip box: "Everyone cheered." Everyone means all the people. "Every one of the cookies was eaten." Every one means each single cookie.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "___ in class passed the test." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
2. Fill in the blank: "___ of the books was checked." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ cheered at the game." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
4. Fill in the blank: "___ of the cookies was eaten." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
5. Fill in the blank: "___ should bring a lunch." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
6. Fill in the blank: "___ of the answers was correct." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ in the hall heard the bell." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
8. Fill in the blank: "___ of the plants got water." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
9. Fill in the blank: "___ laughed at the joke." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
10. Fill in the blank: "___ of the tickets was sold." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
11. Fill in the blank: "___ is invited to the fair." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Everyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.
12. Fill in the blank: "___ of the chairs was in place." Choose everyone or every one. Answer: Every one. Hint: Ask whether the word means all the people, or each single item in a group.

### 15.8 "Anyone" vs. "Any one"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Anyone is one word and means any person at all. Any one is two words and means any single thing or person in a group.
- Example: "Does anyone know the answer?" Anyone means any person at all. "You may pick any one of the prizes." Any one means any single prize.
- How it works: Ask what the words mean. Any person at all means anyone as one word. Any single item in a group means any one as two words.
- Tip box: "Anyone can join the club." Anyone means any person at all. "Any one of these books will do." Any one means any single book.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "Does ___ know the answer?" Choose anyone or any one. Answer: anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
2. Fill in the blank: "You may pick ___ of the prizes." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
3. Fill in the blank: "___ can join the club." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: Anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
4. Fill in the blank: "___ of these books will do." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: Any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
5. Fill in the blank: "Did ___ see the comet?" Choose anyone or any one. Answer: anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
6. Fill in the blank: "Take ___ of the apples." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
7. Fill in the blank: "___ is welcome at the fair." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: Anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
8. Fill in the blank: "Choose ___ of the three paths." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
9. Fill in the blank: "Has ___ tried this game?" Choose anyone or any one. Answer: anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
10. Fill in the blank: "You can borrow ___ of my pens." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
11. Fill in the blank: "___ can learn to code." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: Anyone. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.
12. Fill in the blank: "Pick ___ of the colors." Choose anyone or any one. Answer: any one. Hint: Ask whether the word means any person at all, or any single item in a group.

### 15.9 "Affect" vs. "Effect"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Affect is usually a verb. It means to change or influence something. Effect is usually a noun. It means the result of a change.
- Example: "The rain will affect our picnic." Affect is the verb for changing the picnic. "The effect of the rain was a wet field." Effect is the noun for the result.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. Changing or influencing means affect, the verb. The result means effect, the noun.
- Tip box: "Affect is the action, effect is the end result." A memory trick: affect starts with a, like action. "The new rules affected the game." "The effect was clear."

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "The rain will ___ our picnic plans." Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
2. Fill in the blank: "The ___ of the rain was a wet field." Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
3. Fill in the blank: "Loud noise can ___ your hearing." Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
4. Fill in the blank: "One ___ of practice is skill." Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
5. Fill in the blank: "The new rules ___ the game." Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
6. Fill in the blank: "The ___ was clear to everyone." Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
7. Fill in the blank: "Sleep can ___ how well you learn." Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
8. Fill in the blank: "What is the ___ of eating too much sugar?" Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
9. Fill in the blank: "The storm did not ___ the library." Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
10. Fill in the blank: "The main ___ of the law was safer roads." Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
11. Fill in the blank: "How does music ___ your mood?" Choose affect or effect. Answer: affect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.
12. Fill in the blank: "The ___ of the medicine was quick." Choose affect or effect. Answer: effect. Hint: Ask whether the blank needs a verb for changing something or a noun for the result.

### 15.10 "Accept" vs. "Except"

**Teaching card**
- Definition: Accept means to take or agree to something. Except means leaving something out, or apart from.
- Example: "Please accept this gift." Accept means to take it. "Everyone came except Leo." Except means Leo was left out.
- How it works: Ask what the word does. Taking or agreeing means accept. Leaving out means except.
- Tip box: "I accept your apology." Accept means to take it. "All the cookies are gone except one." Except means one is left out.

**Quiz**
1. Fill in the blank: "Please ___ this gift." Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
2. Fill in the blank: "Everyone came ___ Leo." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
3. Fill in the blank: "I ___ your apology." Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
4. Fill in the blank: "All the cookies are gone ___ one." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
5. Fill in the blank: "Will you ___ the job?" Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
6. Fill in the blank: "Everything is ready ___ the posters." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
7. Fill in the blank: "She will ___ the award on Friday." Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
8. Fill in the blank: "No one knew the answer ___ Ana." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
9. Fill in the blank: "He cannot ___ the gift." Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
10. Fill in the blank: "All the students passed ___ one." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
11. Fill in the blank: "Please ___ my thanks." Choose accept or except. Answer: accept. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.
12. Fill in the blank: "We invited everyone ___ the twins." Choose accept or except. Answer: except. Hint: Ask whether the word means to take or agree to something, or means leaving something out.

## Pathway 17: Components of a Sentence I (5 topics)

### 17.1 Identifying the Subject of a Sentence

**Teaching card**
- Definition: The subject is who or what the sentence is about. It tells who does the action or who is described.
- Example: "The cat slept on the rug." The subject is cat, because the sentence is about the cat.
- How it works: Ask who or what the sentence is about. The answer is the subject. Then find the verb, and check that the subject does it or is described by it.
- Tip box: "Maya and Leo built a fort." The subject is Maya and Leo. "The tall trees swayed." The subject is trees.

**Quiz**
1. "The cat slept on the rug." What is the subject? Answer: cat. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
2. "Maya and Leo built a fort." What is the subject? Answer: Maya and Leo. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
3. "The tall trees swayed in the wind." What is the subject? Answer: trees. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
4. "A robin sang at dawn." What is the subject? Answer: robin. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
5. "The old bridge crossed the river." What is the subject? Answer: bridge. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
6. "Three friends shared a pizza." What is the subject? Answer: friends. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
7. "The library opens at nine." What is the subject? Answer: library. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
8. "A loud storm woke the town." What is the subject? Answer: storm. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
9. "The soccer team won the final game." What is the subject? Answer: team. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
10. "My little brother hid under the bed." What is the subject? Answer: brother. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
11. "The red car stopped at the light." What is the subject? Answer: car. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.
12. "Five ducks swam across the pond." What is the subject? Answer: ducks. Hint: Ask who or what the sentence is about.

### 17.2 Identifying Pronouns as Subjects

**Teaching card**
- Definition: A pronoun can be the subject of a sentence. Subject pronouns are I, you, he, she, it, we, and they. They take the place of a noun.
- Example: "Ana is tall. She plays basketball." She is the subject pronoun that takes the place of Ana.
- How it works: Find the subject, then ask if it is a pronoun. If the word is I, you, he, she, it, we, or they, and it does the action, it is a pronoun subject.
- Tip box: "They built a snowman." They is the subject pronoun. "It rained all day." It is the subject pronoun.

**Quiz**
1. "Ana is tall. She plays basketball." What is the pronoun subject in the second sentence? Answer: She. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
2. "They built a snowman." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: They. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
3. "It rained all day." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: It. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
4. "We walked to the park." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: We. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
5. "He fixed the bike." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: He. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
6. "I finished my homework." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: I. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
7. "You did a great job." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: You. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
8. "She read the whole book." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: She. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
9. "It was a cold morning." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: It. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
10. "They won the game." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: They. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
11. "We are going to the fair." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: We. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.
12. "He is my best friend." What is the pronoun subject? Answer: He. Hint: Find who or what the sentence is about, then check whether that word is a pronoun such as I, you, he, she, it, we, or they.

### 17.3 Identifying the Verb in a Sentence

**Teaching card**
- Definition: The verb is the action word or the state of being in a sentence. It tells what the subject does or is.
- Example: "The dog barked at the mail carrier." Barked is the verb. It tells what the dog does.
- How it works: Find the subject, then ask what the subject does or is. The answer is the verb.
- Tip box: "Maya jumped over the puddle." Jumped is the verb. "The sky is blue." Is is the verb, a state of being.

**Quiz**
1. "The dog barked at the mail carrier." What is the verb? Answer: barked. Hint: The verb tells what the dog does.
2. "Maya jumped over the puddle." What is the verb? Answer: jumped. Hint: The verb tells what Maya does.
3. "The sky is blue." What is the verb? Answer: is. Hint: Look for the word that links the sky to its color.
4. "Leo read a book about space." What is the verb? Answer: read. Hint: The verb tells what Leo does.
5. "The flowers grew in the garden." What is the verb? Answer: grew. Hint: The verb tells what the flowers do.
6. "Ana was happy about the news." What is the verb? Answer: was. Hint: Look for the word that links Ana to her feeling about the news.
7. "The train arrived on time." What is the verb? Answer: arrived. Hint: The verb tells what the train does.
8. "Ben cooked dinner for his family." What is the verb? Answer: cooked. Hint: The verb tells what Ben does.
9. "The baby slept all night." What is the verb? Answer: slept. Hint: The verb tells what the baby does.
10. "The river flows through the valley." What is the verb? Answer: flows. Hint: The verb tells what the river does.
11. "Zoe felt tired after the hike." What is the verb? Answer: felt. Hint: Look for the word that links Zoe to her tired feeling.
12. "The students built a robot." What is the verb? Answer: built. Hint: The verb tells what the students do.

### 17.4 Identifying Verbs and Their Direct Objects

**Teaching card**
- Definition: A direct object is the noun or pronoun that receives the action of the verb. It answers the question what or whom after the verb.
- Example: "Maya kicked the ball." Ball is the direct object. It answers what Maya kicked.
- How it works: Find the verb, then ask what or whom. The answer is the direct object.
- Tip box: "Leo read a book." Book is the direct object. It answers what Leo read. "Ana called her friend." Friend is the direct object. It answers whom Ana called.

**Quiz**
1. "Maya kicked the ball." What is the direct object? Answer: ball. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Maya kicked.
2. "Leo read a book." What is the direct object? Answer: book. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Leo read.
3. "Ana called her friend." What is the direct object? Answer: friend. Hint: Find the verb, then ask whom Ana called.
4. "The cat caught the mouse." What is the direct object? Answer: mouse. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what the cat caught.
5. "Ben built a fort." What is the direct object? Answer: fort. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Ben built.
6. "Zoe painted a picture." What is the direct object? Answer: picture. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Zoe painted.
7. "The gardener watered the plants." What is the direct object? Answer: plants. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what the gardener watered.
8. "Sam wrote a letter." What is the direct object? Answer: letter. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Sam wrote.
9. "The teacher graded the tests." What is the direct object? Answer: tests. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what the teacher graded.
10. "Maya fed the dog." What is the direct object? Answer: dog. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Maya fed.
11. "Leo fixed the bike." What is the direct object? Answer: bike. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Leo fixed.
12. "Ana baked cookies." What is the direct object? Answer: cookies. Hint: Find the verb, then ask what Ana baked.

### 17.5 Identifying Prepositions

**Teaching card**
- Definition: A preposition is a word that shows the link between a noun or pronoun and another word in the sentence. Common prepositions are in, on, under, over, beside, behind, between, through, and with.
- Example: "The cat hid under the bed." Under is the preposition. It shows the link between cat and bed.
- How it works: Look for a word that tells where, when, or how things relate. A preposition starts a phrase that ends with a noun or pronoun.
- Tip box: "The book is on the table." On shows where the book is. "We walked through the park." Through shows where we walked.

**Quiz**
1. "The cat hid under the bed." Which word is the preposition? Answer: under. Hint: Look for the word that shows the link between cat and bed.
2. "The book is on the table." Which word is the preposition? Answer: on. Hint: Look for the word that shows where the book is.
3. "We walked through the park." Which word is the preposition? Answer: through. Hint: Look for the word that shows where we walked.
4. "Ana sat beside her friend." Which word is the preposition? Answer: beside. Hint: Look for the word that shows the link between Ana and her friend.
5. "The bird flew over the house." Which word is the preposition? Answer: over. Hint: Look for the word that shows where the bird flew.
6. "Leo hid behind the tree." Which word is the preposition? Answer: behind. Hint: Look for the word that shows where Leo hid.
7. "The ball rolled between the chairs." Which word is the preposition? Answer: between. Hint: Look for the word that shows where the ball rolled.
8. "Maya walked home with her brother." Which word is the preposition? Answer: with. Hint: Look for the word that shows the link between Maya and her brother.
9. "The keys are in the drawer." Which word is the preposition? Answer: in. Hint: Look for the word that shows where the keys are.
10. "We met at noon." Which word is the preposition? Answer: at. Hint: Look for the word that shows when we met.
11. "The dog ran across the field." Which word is the preposition? Answer: across. Hint: Look for the word that shows where the dog ran.
12. "She wrote a note for her teacher." Which word is the preposition? Answer: for. Hint: Look for the word that shows the link between note and teacher.
